```text
790	WildChat FPR by turn count (Part~B) provides complementary evidence: short conversations (3--10 turns, $n$=196) have \emph{lower} FPR than medium-length ones (11--15 turns, $n$=23), inconsistent with a ``short\,=\,jailbreak'' shortcut. Medium and long buckets have limited sample sizes and should be interpreted as directional evidence only.
791	
792	Padding jailbreak conversations with benign turns (Part~C) reveals an asymmetry: DD detection is resilient ($\Delta$=$-$2.5\,pp), but ActorAttack collapses ($\Delta$=$-$57.9\,pp). Padding experiments append benign turns \emph{after} the complete attack sequence; in practice, adversaries would not dilute their own successful attacks. The collapse nonetheless exposes BiGRU temporal aggregation as vulnerable to attention dilution on subtle attack signals, a limitation discussed in Section~\ref{sec:limitations}.
793	
794	\section{Inference Efficiency}
795	\label{app:efficiency}
796	
797	\begin{table}[ht]
798	\centering
799	\footnotesize
800	\caption{Inference efficiency comparison. DeBERTa+BiGRU and TF-IDF+LR measured on IID test (76 conversations, avg 8.3 turns, single A100). LLM latency from separate OOD evaluation (118 conversations; exp10); cross-condition comparison is indicative only.}
801	\label{tab:efficiency}
802	\begin{tabular}{@{}lrcr@{}}
803	\toprule
804	\textbf{Method} & Latency (ms) & Params & VRAM \\
805	\midrule
806	TF-IDF+LR & 0.43$\pm$0.05 & $\sim$10K & CPU \\
807	DeBERTa+BiGRU (ours) & 12.1$\pm$1.2 & 186.7M & 862\,MB \\
808	Qwen3-8B 3-shot$^{\dagger}$ & $\sim$5{,}200 & 8B & $\sim$16\,GB \\
809	\bottomrule
810	\end{tabular}
811	\vspace{-2pt}
812	{\footnotesize $^{\dagger}$Qwen3-8B latency from exp10 (DD/AA OOD, 118 conversations); not directly comparable to encoder latency measured on IID test.}
813	\end{table}
814	
815	Table~\ref{tab:efficiency} compares inference cost. On matched data (IID test), DeBERTa+BiGRU processes a conversation in 12.1\,ms, 28$\times$ slower than TF-IDF (0.43\,ms), but this speed gap purchases +71\,pp on ActorAttack (F1\,=\,0.972 vs.\ 0.261; Table~\ref{tab:evidence_hierarchy}). LLM-as-judge approaches are orders of magnitude slower ($\sim$5.2\,s per conversation for Qwen3-8B 3-shot) while achieving substantially lower OOD detection (DD\,=\,0.566, AA\,=\,0.652; Table~\ref{tab:llm_judge}). Zero-shot Qwen3-8B achieves F1\,=\,0.0 on DD OOD because it predicts all conversations as benign (Table~\ref{tab:llm_judge}). The full DeBERTa+BiGRU pipeline requires 862\,MB GPU memory, enabling deployment on consumer hardware.
816	
817	\section{Data Efficiency}
818	\label{app:data_efficiency}
819	
820	\begin{table}[ht]
821	\centering
822	\footnotesize
823	\caption{Data efficiency of 9-class DAPT (3-seed mean$\pm$std, sample std with $n{-}1$ divisor) and vanilla baseline (seed 42 only). $N$ = total training conversations (balanced jailbreak/benign).}
824	\label{tab:data_efficiency}

    "originalFile": "% introduction.tex\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} all rely on persuasion as their core mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} identify strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Because individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, no single-turn filter can distinguish them from legitimate conversation.\n\nExisting defenses leave a critical gap. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and miss multi-turn manipulation; Llama Guard~3 detects only 1.8\\% of multi-turn jailbreaks in our evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:decomposition}). Multi-turn defenses (DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026}, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid}, G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025}) model conversation-level structure but each evaluates on a single attack family. None answers the central question: \\emph{what representation properties enable a detector to generalize across attack families it has never seen?}\n\nPretrained language understanding does not suffice at base model scale: vanilla DeBERTa-v3~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation (cf.\\ DeepContext) drops from F1\\,=\\,1.000 in-distribution to 0.312 on OOD Deceptive Delight, and GPT-5.5 reaches only 0.191--0.449 F1 (across prefix lengths) with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). The bottleneck is distributional: cross-attack generalization requires domain adaptation, not a better classifier.\n\nOur pipeline, \\textbf{DisPeD} (Disentangled Persuasion Detection), separates encoder adaptation (Stage~1) from temporal classification (Stage~2, a frozen BiGRU; Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture}). Seven Stage~1 variants each isolate a single factor while holding all others constant (Section~\\ref{sec:method}); the contribution is the decomposition, not the pipeline.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}\n\\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \\texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}\n\\label{fig:architecture}\n\\end{figure}\n\nControlled ablation across DeBERTa-v3 and RoBERTa~\\citep{liu2019roberta} on four attack families and two training compositions shows that for base-size encoders, classification-based adaptation is the most robust variant on synthetic multi-turn OOD attacks. MLM collapses on mixed-LLM training (F1\\,=\\,0.289 $\\approx$ vanilla) while classification DAPT maintains 0.818; RoBERTa-large (355M) handles ActorAttack~\\citep{ren2024actorattack} without adaptation. Classification supervision is associated with substantially lower prediction variance on synthetic data but not on human-authored conversations (Section~\\ref{sec:human_data}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Systematic ablation of cross-attack generalization factors.} On synthetic multi-turn data, thirteen encoder configurations at three scales (125--355M) decompose jailbreak detection into two orthogonal axes (attack-side and benign-side), the first such controlled factorial study in this domain. On the attack side, classification DAPT recovers OOD detection (vanilla 0.26--0.31 $\\rightarrow$ 0.97--1.0) while unsupervised pretraining collapses under mixed-LLM training. On the benign side, 10--25 target-domain conversations reduce FPR from 58--68\\% to 0--1.5\\% on two independent sources (WildChat, OASST2; IID evaluation) without detection degradation.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Partition sufficiency with an observed difficulty gradient (at base model scale).} Domain-relevant classification objectives, including scrambled labels, suffice for cross-attack generalization on template attacks (TOST-confirmed, $d < 0.1$ on DD/FITD at $n$\\,=\\,10), while attacks employing more varied conversational strategies (ActorAttack) show a moderate label effect ($d$\\,=\\,0.44). This is consistent with \\citet{zhang2021understanding}'s framework: domain-relevant partitioning is necessary (topic labels fail) and compression localizes to 3 SVD dimensions in layers 8--12. A probe over these dimensions confirms sufficiency; removing them degrades DD by 17\\,pp while FITD is unaffected (partial necessity; Appendix~\\ref{app:discriminative_probe}).\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Two-step adaptation protocol.} On the synthetic benchmark, continued pretraining achieves 24.3$\\times$ inference speedup over LLM-as-judge (a deployment cost comparison; parameter counts differ by 43$\\times$; Appendix~\\ref{app:efficiency}); 10--25 target-domain benign conversations reduce FPR below deployment thresholds on both WildChat and OASST2 crowdsourced conversations. No target-domain attack data is required.\n\\end{itemize}\n",

I'm noticing the cross-encoder results show RoBERTa-base performs much better on in-distribution data than DeBERTa, achieving 0.981 with vanilla settings, so domain adaptation adds minimal gains—but ActorAttack reveals this masks brittleness, with a dramatic 51.5 percentage point collapse on adversarial examples.

Scaling up to RoBERTa-large flips the pattern entirely: the largest model doesn't need adaptation for attacks and actually degrades when pretrained further, suggesting representation drift becomes problematic at scale. This creates a clear encoder-dependent hierarchy where smaller models require domain adaptation to handle complex attacks, while larger ones don't, and classification supervision consistently stabilizes performance across both base encoders.

A BERT-base proxy confirms that cross-attack generalization comes from the domain adaptation strategy itself rather than architectural differences. CKA analysis shows DAPT substantially reshapes the representation space, though the magnitude of these shifts doesn't always correlate with detection improvements.

I should trim the CKA detail since it's just supporting evidence, and compress the BERT proxy explanation. Let me also check if the mixed-LLM section has any redundancy I can cut.

_source line 7504 · 2026-05-23T07:02:52.569Z_

[Director 指令] **Figure 1 再次重画 — 用户要求"高大上"、"档次高"，当前版本太简单**

当前版本逻辑正确但视觉太 easy——本质上就是几个方框加箭头，和课件图没区别。顶会论文的主图需要有信息密度、视觉层次和设计感。

## 设计升级方向

当前图的问题：只展示了抽象的模块名（DeBERTa → BiGRU → Softmax），没有展示**数据是怎么流动的**。顶会主图的共同特点是**让读者一眼看到数据变换过程**，而不只是模块名。

### 新设计核心思路：展示 turn-by-turn 数据流

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                                         │
│   Multi-turn          Stage 1:                    Stage 2:              │
│   Conversation        Encoder Adaptation          Temporal Classification│
│                                                                         │
│   ┌──────────┐        ┌─────────────┐                                  │
│   │ "Can you  │──────▶│  DeBERTa    │──▶ [h₁]─┐                       │
│   │  help..." │        │  Encoder    │          │   ┌───────┐           │
│   └──────────┘        └─────────────┘          ├──▶│       │  ┌─────┐  │
│   ┌──────────┐        ┌─────────────┐          │   │ BiGRU │─▶│ MLP │──▶ ⚠️/✓│
│   │ "What if  │──────▶│  DeBERTa    │──▶ [h₂]─┤   │       │  └─────┘  │
│   │  we..."   │        │  Encoder    │          │   └───────┘           │
│   └──────────┘        └─────────────┘          │                       │
│       ⋮                    ⋮                    │                       │
│   ┌──────────┐        ┌─────────────┐          │                       │
│   │ "Just do  │──────▶│  DeBERTa    │──▶ [hₖ]─┘                       │
│   │  it..."   │        │  Encoder    │                                  │
│   └──────────┘        └─────────────┘                                  │
│                       ×7 training objectives                            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

关键改进点：
1. **展示 per-turn processing**：每个 user turn 单独经过 DeBERTa，提取 [CLS] embedding。不是一个大框处理所有 turns，而是**明确展示 turn-by-turn 的编码过程**
2. **embedding 序列可视化**：[h₁], [h₂], ..., [hₖ] 用小的渐变色条/向量块表示，汇聚到 BiGRU——这展示了 temporal modeling 的核心思想
3. **对话气泡**：左侧用对话气泡/消息框样式（而非简单矩形），立即传达"多轮对话"的语境
4. **共享权重标注**：多个 DeBERTa 框之间用虚线连接或用"shared weights"标注，表示同一个编码器
5. **×7 variants 集成**：在 DeBERTa 框旁边用小的图标/badge 标注训练目标变体

## PaperBanana Intent

用 Google API key 调用 PaperBanana，**intent 如下**（可在此基础上优化）：

```
Sophisticated academic architecture diagram for EMNLP top-conference paper, showing a two-stage multi-turn jailbreak detection pipeline with explicit per-turn data flow.

OVERALL: Left-to-right flow, ~1600px wide, ~600px tall. White background. Three visual zones with rich internal structure. This must look like a best-paper-award figure with high information density.

LEFT ZONE — MULTI-TURN CONVERSATION (~20%):
Show 3 conversation message bubbles stacked vertically, styled like chat messages (rounded rectangles with a small triangle/tail on the left side, like iMessage or WhatsApp). Each bubble contains abbreviated example text in light gray italic 8pt:
- Bubble 1: "Can you help me with..." (labeled User₁)
- Bubble 2: "What if we consider..." (labeled User₂)  
- Between bubble 2 and 3: vertical ellipsis (⋮)
- Bubble 3: "So just do it then..." (labeled Userₖ)
Bubbles fill: #F8F9FA, border: #DEE2E6, slight rounded corners. Each bubble is connected to its own encoder instance by a thin arrow.

MIDDLE ZONE — STAGE 1: ENCODER ADAPTATION (~40%):
Title "Stage 1: Encoder Adaptation" in bold at top.
Show the DeBERTa encoder as a VERTICAL STACK of transformer layers (3-4 thin horizontal bars stacked inside a rounded rectangle, suggesting depth/layers). This encoder is shown THREE TIMES vertically aligned with the three input bubbles, with dashed lines between the three instances labeled "shared weights" to indicate weight sharing.

Each encoder instance outputs a small colored embedding vector block [h₁], [h₂], [hₖ] — show these as small filled rectangles with a gradient (blue to purple), labeled with subscript. These embedding blocks flow rightward.

Below the encoder stack: compact annotation "×7 training objectives" with a subtle expandable indicator, and in tiny 7pt text: "9-Class · Binary · Scrambled · MLM-only · Wiki-MLM · Topic · Vanilla"

Annotation at bottom: "frozen [CLS] embeddings" in italic gray.

RIGHT ZONE — STAGE 2: TEMPORAL CLASSIFICATION (~30%):
Title "Stage 2: Temporal Classification" in bold at top.
The three embedding vectors [h₁], [h₂], [hₖ] feed into a BiGRU block as a SEQUENCE (show them entering sequentially with a time arrow t→). BiGRU shown as a rounded rectangle with bidirectional arrows inside (suggesting recurrent processing).

BiGRU outputs to an MLP block (smaller rectangle), then to a binary output:
- "Jailbreak" with a warning icon (⚠️) in warm coral (#E8634A)
- "Benign" with a checkmark (✓) in calm teal (#2E8B7A)

Annotation: "shared across all 7 variants" in italic gray.

VISUAL STYLE:
- Color palette: muted academic — soft blues (#4A90D9, #B8D4E8), soft purples (#8B7EC8), warm grays (#F0EDE8), calm greens (#E8F0E8). No pure primary colors.
- All boxes: rounded corners (8-12px), subtle drop shadow (2px, alpha 0.1)
- Arrows: dark gray (#555), thin (1.5pt), with small arrowheads
- Typography: clean sans-serif, clear hierarchy (titles 13pt bold, labels 11pt, annotations 8-9pt italic gray)
- Show internal structure (transformer layers, bidirectional arrows) rather than plain boxes
- 300 DPI, publication quality
```

## 执行

1. 用 PaperBanana + Google API key：`GOOGLE_API_KEY="[REDACTED]"`
2. 不需要代理，直接调用
3. iterations=5
4. 如果 PaperBanana CLI 不行，走方案 D（直接调 Gemini API 生成 matplotlib 代码 + VLM 迭代）
5. 生成后自己检查：
   - 逻辑是否与 method.tex 一致（per-turn encoding → temporal sequence → classification）
   - 7 个变体名称正确
   - 视觉是否真正"高大上"——如果还是普通方框图就继续迭代
6. PNG (300 DPI) + PDF → `figures/paper/fig1_architecture.{png,pdf}`
7. **禁止修改任何 .tex 文件**

**核心区别**：这次不是画"模块图"，是画"数据流图"——读者要能看到每个 turn 是怎么独立编码、怎么变成 embedding 序列、怎么被 BiGRU 处理的。

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The figure looks significantly better than the previous version:
- ✅ Per-turn data flow: 3 user turns each going through their own DeBERTa instance
- ✅ Chat-like message bubbles with example text
- ✅ Embedding vectors [h₁], [h₂], [hₖ] shown as colored blocks
- ✅ "shared weights" annotation between encoder instances
- ✅ DeBERTa shows internal structure (transformer layers as horizontal bars)
- ✅ BiGRU with bidirectional arrows


- ✅ MLP feeding into classification outputs with warning and checkmark icons
- ✅ Frozen [CLS] embeddings clearly marked
- ✅ All 7 training objectives labeled with shared weights annotation across variants
- ✅ Time sequence arrow for input progression
- ✅ Muted academic color scheme throughout

This is a major step up from the earlier diagram—it actually depicts the per-turn processing pipeline and shows how data flows through the shared encoder architecture.

Now I'm double-checking the variant names against the paper: 9-Class, Binary, Scrambled, MLM-only, Wiki-MLM, Topic, and Vanilla all check out. "Binary" is technically "Binary Persuasion" in the paper, but the abbreviated form works fine for space constraints in a figure. Let me render this to make sure it compiles properly.
```

_source line 7572 · 2026-05-23T08:30:52.159Z_

[Director 指令] **GPU 不能空着。跑一个 rebuttal 预存实验：第三 benign 平台适配。**

## 实验设计

**假设**：9-class encoder 在第三个独立 benign 数据源上，few-shot adaptation (n=10-25) 同样能将 FPR 降至接近 0%，加强 Claim 3 的泛化性。

**数据源选择**（按可获取性排序）：
1. ShareGPT（优先）— 多轮对话格式，与 WildChat/OASST2 分布不同
2. LMSYS-Chat-1M — 备选，量大但需要过滤

**实验内容**：
- 用已有的 9-class DeBERTa-v3-base encoder
- 在新 benign 数据源上做 few-shot adaptation（n=5, 10, 15, 20, 25）
- 测量 FPR 变化曲线
- 与 WildChat/OASST2 结果对比

**预估时间**：1-2 GPU hours（数据下载 + 预处理 + 5 个 few-shot 设置的评估）

**来源**：Reviewer 最终评估推荐的最高 ROI 预跑实验，为 rebuttal 预存结果。对应 Claim 3（two-step protocol）的泛化性验证。

**服务器**：westd-33183，GPU 由 Director 分配。

提交实验方案后等我审批。

eliminating the data leakage concern in exp54's causal subspace analysis\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp53_llm_judge_prompt_opt\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"An optimized CoT prompt with structured reasoning steps and calibrated examples will improve LLM-as-Judge jailbreak detection F1, especially on OOD datasets (DD, AA), compared to the original 3-shot prompt\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp55_5seed_mlm_vs_9class_aa\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class persuasion-grounded DeBERTa DAPT produces significantly better OOD generalization than domain-exposure-only MLM DAPT, with the advantage being robust across 5 random seeds (expanding from 3 to address reviewer W2 concern about insufficient seed count)\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp56_crossllm_10shot_adaptation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"10-shot benign adaptation with Llama-origin conversations will reduce cross-LLM FPR from 88-90% to below 10%, extending the two-axis benign adaptation mechanism to cross-LLM distributional shift\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp54_causal_probe_subspace\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Classification DAPT compresses representations into few discriminative dimensions that are causally necessary for OOD generalization - projecting onto top-k SVD components preserves detection while random/vanilla projections fail\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp52_oasst2_n25_multiseed\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"OASST2 benign adaptation with N=25 yields consistently low FPR (≤5%) across multiple seeds (42, 123, 456), confirming seed 42's FPR=1.5% is not a lucky outlier.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp51_lmsys_benign_ood\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"The two-axis decomposition (high FPR on OOD benign + 10-shot remediation) generalizes beyond WildChat: LMSYS-Chat-1M benign conversations will show high FPR (>20%) that 10 target-domain conversations can eliminate, confirming benign-side shift is a general phenomenon not specific to WildChat GPT-3.5 users.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp50_layerwise_representation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Discriminative compression from classification DAPT concentrates in DeBERTa's later layers (7-12) rather than early layers (1-6), mechanistically distinguishing it from Maennel et al.'s early-layer label-independent statistics. If PR and Fisher ratio diverge between classification-supervised and MLM/vanilla only in later layers, this confirms compression is a task-specific representation restructuring, not an early-layer phenomenon.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp45_benign_adaptation_curve\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Adding N WildChat benign samples (N in {0,5,10,25,50,100,150}) to training data will show a monotonic FPR reduction curve, with substantial FPR drop achievable with as few as 10-25 samples while maintaining OOD F1 above 0.95.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp49_representation_analysis\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Classification-based DAPT (9-class, binary, scrambled) produces smaller weight perturbations from pretrained weights and more geometrically uniform representations (higher effective dimensionality) than MLM DAPT, consistent with optimization regularization as the primary mechanism for cross-attack robustness.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp48_ensemble_mlm_9class\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Ensembling JB-MLM (strongest on ToxicChat F1=0.837) and 9-class (strongest on synthetic OOD) will outperform either individual model across all benchmarks, because their error patterns are complementary: MLM captures general linguistic anomaly while 9-class captures persuasion-strategy-specific features.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp46\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"DisPeD (DeBERTa+BiGRU) achieves significantly lower inference latency than Qwen3-8B LLM-as-judge while maintaining superior accuracy, because the specialized two-stage pipeline avoids autoregressive decoding overhead.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp44_multi_llm_mixed\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Mixed multi-LLM training (Qwen+Llama jailbreak data) maintains evidence hierarchy and OOD detection on cross-LLM test while preserving Qwen OOD F1 above 0.95, because persuasion strategy patterns are LLM-agnostic features\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp43_mhj_human_eval\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class DAPT representations encode transferable persuasion signals that generalize to real human multi-turn jailbreak attempts (MHJ dataset), achieving higher detection recall than vanilla DeBERTa because human attackers use similar persuasion strategies as synthetic attacks.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp42b_balanced_ratio_ablation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"exp42's WildChat FPR reduction (68.1%→0%) is driven by benign distribution coverage, not the shifted class ratio (50:50→65:35). With balanced 50:50 ratio (88 WC benign + 87 orig benign vs 175 jailbreak), FPR should still drop to near 0%.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp42_ood_benign_mixed\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Including WildChat benign conversations in training data will reduce WildChat FPR from 68.1% to below 30% while maintaining jailbreak OOD detection F1 above 0.95 on DD/AA/FITD, because the current high FPR is driven by benign distribution mismatch (exp14c: 100% from FP explosion), not model architecture limitations. Mixed benign training should expand the model's benign representation space to cover real-world conversational diversity.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp41_tost_10seed\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Extending TOST equivalence test from n=5 to n=10 seeds will resolve the inconclusive AA result (p=0.08 at n=5) by providing sufficient statistical power: if scrambled and 9-class are truly equivalent within 5pp margin on ActorAttack, the increased sample size will yield p<0.05, confirming label content irrelevance across all OOD families.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp40_llama_guard_baseline\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Llama Guard 3 8B（single-turn 安全分类器）在多轮 jailbreak 检测上表现远低于 DisPeD，因为它缺乏多轮对话的时序建模能力和说服策略感知，验证 multi-turn 场景需要专门适配的核心 claim。\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp40\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Llama Guard 3 8B, as a general-purpose safety classifier, will underperform on multi-turn persuasion-based jailbreak detection compared to our specialized turn-level model, particularly on early detection (K=2) and OOD attack families, because it lacks turn-level persuasion strategy awareness.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp39_extra_seeds_tost\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"扩展 9-class 和 scrambled 到 5 seeds (新增 789/1000) 将提供足够统计效力支持 TOST 等价性检验：scrambled 在 DD OOD 上与 9-class 等价（差距 &lt;2pp），但在 AA OOD 上显著更低，证明策略标签的正确语义信息对 hard OOD 至关重要。\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp38_wildcat_calibration\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"少量 WildChat benign 样本 (~50 条) 即可通过阈值校准将 FPR 降至 <25% 且 TPR >90%，证明 domain calibration 是可行的 practical path，WildChat 高 FPR 是阈值问题而非表征能力问题。\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp37_length_matched_benign\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class 模型不依赖 turn count 作为检测 shortcut：截断 benign 对话到 jailbreak 匹配长度 (3-10 turns) 后 FPR 不会显著上升，WildChat 短对话 (3-10 turns) 的 FPR 也不高于长对话，因为 DAPT 训练迫使模型学习说服策略内容特征而非 length 统计量。\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp36\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"MLM DAPT encoder produces weaker frozen representations than classification (9-class) DAPT encoder for OOD jailbreak detection, because classification training forces the encoder to learn task-relevant discriminative features while MLM only learns general language patterns.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp35_cross_seed_ensemble\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"3-seed majority vote ensemble 将在所有 OOD 攻击家族上提升检测 F1（尤其减少 AA OOD 上的漏检），9-class ensemble 的提升幅度大于 MLM/vanilla，因为 9-class 各 seed 的错误模式更互补（来自不同的策略表征空间初始化）。\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp34_representation_tsne\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class DAPT 的 CLS embedding 在 t-SNE/UMAP 2D 投影中将显示 jailbreak/benign 更清晰的分离（尤其在 AA OOD 上），而 vanilla 和 MLM 的表征将呈现更多重叠，因为 9-class 的说服策略预训练迫使编码器学习与攻击意图相关的判别性特征。\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp33_strategy_prediction_plan002\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Plan_002 checkpoints (主管道, AA OOD ~97%) 的策略预测-检测关联将比 exp32 (exp16 mixed-LLM checkpoints) 更强，因为 plan_002 在 AA OOD 上检测率更高，正确检测样本的策略预测准确率与检测概率的相关性也应更显著。\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp28c_vanilla_supplement\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Vanilla DeBERTa (no DAPT) baseline at N=50 and N=250 will show substantially lower OOD F1 compared to 9-class DAPT, completing the data efficiency curve and confirming that DAPT benefit persists across all training sizes.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp32_strategy_prediction\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Correctly detected jailbreak conversations have higher persuasion strategy prediction accuracy than missed ones, and certain strategy types (e.g., rapport_building, emotional_manipulation) are associated with higher detection rates, because the persuasion head's ability to identify strategies is mechanistically linked to the encoder's discriminative power for downstream detection.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp31_lambda_ablation_aa\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Multi-task persuasion loss weight (lambda) affects OOD generalization in E2E fine-tuning: lambda=0 (intent-only) will underperform on hard OOD (ActorAttack) compared to lambda>0, because the persuasion auxiliary signal provides a regularization effect that preserves OOD-relevant features in the encoder. There exists an optimal lambda that maximizes AA OOD without sacrificing IID performance.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp28b_data_efficiency_supplement\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class DAPT maintains robust OOD performance across different random seeds (model initialization), and vanilla DeBERTa shows poor OOD generalization even at smaller training sizes (N=50, N=250), confirming that the data efficiency advantage of 9-class DAPT is not an artifact of a single seed.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp29_inference_efficiency\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"DeBERTa+BiGRU achieves orders-of-magnitude faster inference than LLM-based judges while maintaining superior OOD detection accuracy, demonstrating practical deployability for real-time jailbreak detection.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp28_data_efficiency\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class DAPT maintains strong OOD generalization even at 25% training data (n=50-125), outperforming vanilla baseline at 100% data (n=350), demonstrating high sample efficiency of persuasion-grounded representations.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp30_length_stratified\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class DAPT maintains consistent jailbreak detection performance across different conversation lengths (turn counts), with no systematic degradation in shorter or longer conversations.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp27_mean_pooling\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Mean pooling (no temporal modeling) with end-to-end fine-tuning will match BiGRU E2E on easy OOD (DD) but degrade on hard OOD (ActorAttack), demonstrating that temporal modeling via BiGRU is necessary for detecting multi-turn adaptive attacks.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp26_frozen_probing\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Frozen 9-class DAPT DeBERTa representations alone carry sufficient OOD generalization signal: a randomly-initialized BiGRU trained on these frozen embeddings should achieve comparable OOD F1 to the jointly-trained plan_002 GRU, because the DAPT pre-training encodes persuasion-strategy structure that transfers across attack types.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp24_calibration_ece\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"The 9-class multi-task model produces better-calibrated confidence scores than vanilla DeBERTa+BiGRU, especially under OOD distribution shift, because persuasion-aware features provide more grounded probability estimates.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp22_aa_early_detection\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class DAPT maintains detection advantage over MLM and vanilla at all prefix lengths (K=1,2,3,5,full) on ActorAttack OOD, with the gap widening at smaller K, demonstrating early detection capability on hard OOD attacks\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp25_fitd_early_detection\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class persuasion-aware model detects FITD jailbreaks earlier (higher F1 at low K) than MLM and vanilla baselines, similar to the pattern observed on ActorAttack OOD.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp23_aa_error_analysis\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class DAPT errors on AA OOD are concentrated in specific topics/strategies, with short-turn conversations being harder to detect\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp21_cross_checkpoint\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Downstream OOD performance is insensitive to Stage-1 DAPT training variation: different DAPT seeds should yield cross-checkpoint F1 std < 2pp on IID, DD OOD, and AA OOD benchmarks.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp20_deepcontext_proxy\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"BERT-base-uncased + BiGRU (frozen probing, no DAPT) as DeepContext proxy will substantially underperform DeBERTa-v3 9-class on OOD generalization, quantifying the contribution of task-specific DAPT vs generic pre-training and providing a SOTA-comparable baseline for reviewer concern about missing baselines\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp19_wildchat_fpr\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class DAPT classifier maintains FPR < 15% on high-drift real-world benign conversations from WildChat (after excluding jailbreak-contaminated samples), demonstrating external validity beyond synthetic benign data and addressing reviewer concern about deployment false positive rates\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp18_e2e_finetune\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Unfreezing DeBERTa-v3-base encoder for end-to-end fine-tuning (Stage 1 DAPT + Stage 2 GRU joint training) will produce comparable or better OOD F1 vs frozen probing, quantifying the performance gap of the frozen approach and validating probing methodology\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp16_multi_llm\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Training on mixed Qwen+Llama conversations (250+250) eliminates single-LLM confound: the evidence hierarchy (9-class >> MLM >> vanilla) holds on mixed data, and cross-LLM false positive rate drops vs single-source training, proving DAPT generalizes across LLM distributions\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp17_dataset_scale\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Doubling training data from 500 to 1000 conversations (with new topic seeds) will maintain or improve the DAPT evidence hierarchy (9-class >> MLM >> vanilla on OOD), demonstrating that results are not an artifact of small sample size.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp16b_llama_only_mlm\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"MLM DAPT on Llama-only data (250 conversations) will produce stable intermediate performance between vanilla and 9-class on DD OOD and AA OOD, distinguishing whether MLM instability in exp16 mixed training is caused by multi-source data mixing or is inherent to MLM DAPT\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp15c_roberta_large_mlm\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"MLM-only continued pretraining on RoBERTa-large (without classification head) will NOT cause the same performance regression as 9-class DAPT, because the regression stems from classification fine-tuning conflicting with frozen-encoder GRU evaluation, not from domain adaptation itself.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp14c_prediction_analysis\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"exp14 cross-LLM failure is driven by high false positive rate on Llama benign (model classifies unfamiliar Llama benign text as jailbreak). exp14b control should show near-zero FP on Qwen benign with maintained TP on Llama jailbreak, confirming benign distribution coverage as the bottleneck.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp15b_roberta_large_lr\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"exp15 9class underperformance on AA (0.946 vs vanilla 1.000) is caused by catastrophic forgetting from too-high LR (2e-5) for 355M model. Reducing DAPT LR to 1e-5 should preserve pretrained representations while still learning persuasion patterns, recovering AA F1 to match or exceed vanilla.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp15_roberta_large\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"RoBERTa-large (355M) vanilla will still underperform DAPT variants on ActorAttack OOD, demonstrating that DAPT value is independent of model capacity. If RoBERTa-large vanilla matches DAPT performance, then the difficulty threshold is a capacity effect rather than a representation quality effect.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp14b_cross_llm_control\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"exp14 cross-LLM failure is caused by double distribution shift (both jailbreak AND benign from Llama). By pairing Llama jailbreak with Qwen IID benign, we isolate the jailbreak detection component. If F1 recovers significantly, the failure is primarily due to Llama benign misclassification, not inability to detect Llama-style jailbreaks.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp14_cross_llm_validation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"DAPT advantage (9-class/jb_mlm >> vanilla) is not specific to Qwen3-8B-generated data. If the persuasion-grounded fine-tuning captures generalizable jailbreak patterns rather than Qwen-specific artifacts, the performance gap should persist on Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct-generated multi-turn conversations.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp5_llm_judge_ood\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Zero-shot Qwen3-8B will underperform trained classifiers on OOD data (DD and FITD), establishing that even on unseen attack types, specialized trained detectors outperform general-purpose LLM reasoning\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp9_bootstrap_ci\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"BCa bootstrap CIs and paired permutation tests will confirm that multitask 9-class persuasion pre-training significantly outperforms vanilla (frozen) baseline on OOD jailbreak detection, while the difference against domain-specific pre-training (jb_mlm, scrambled) is not statistically significant, supporting the claim that persuasion-aware representations provide robust detection capabilities.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp8_mhj_human_data\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"If the evidence hierarchy (domain-adapted > vanilla) reflects genuine signal learning rather than Qwen3-8B stylistic overfitting, then DeBERTa variants trained on Qwen3-8B data should also outperform vanilla on human-written jailbreak prompts from Reddit/Discord.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp10_fewshot_llm_judge\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"3-shot in-context examples should significantly improve LLM-as-judge jailbreak detection over zero-shot by providing the model with concrete examples of escalation patterns, but will still underperform trained classifiers because few-shot prompting cannot match fine-tuned feature extraction.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp7_tfidf_perprefix_actorattack\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"TF-IDF LR baseline per-prefix results on DD OOD and ActorAttack will show the complete performance profile across K values, filling the gap where only Full F1 was reported, and establishing the full comparison surface against neural methods\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp6_actorattack_perturbation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Classification-supervised variants (9-class/binary/scrambled) will maintain near-zero perturbation degradation on ActorAttack OOD paraphrased data, while vanilla/MLM variants will show significant degradation, extending Claim 3 perturbation robustness from DD to a second OOD attack type\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp2_actorattack_mcnemar\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class persuasion-aware encoder significantly outperforms vanilla DeBERTa on ActorAttack OOD data at per-sample level (McNemar test), supporting cross-attack generalization claim.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp2_actorattack_ood\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Persuasion-grounded DeBERTa will generalize to ActorAttack (a structurally distinct multi-persona attack), extending cross-attack OOD evidence beyond DD and FITD to a third unseen attack type\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp4_dd_ood_perturbation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Persuasion-grounded DeBERTa variants will maintain near-zero perturbation degradation on DD OOD paraphrased data, while vanilla/wiki-MLM variants will show significant degradation, extending IID perturbation robustness findings to OOD setting\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"exp3_roberta_probing\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"RoBERTa 9-class persuasion encoder encodes richer discourse structure than vanilla/MLM variants (matching DeBERTa pattern), as measured by linear probing accuracy and CKA divergence, confirming findings are encoder-agnostic\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"vanilla_multiseed_dd_ood\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"DeBERTa vanilla (no Stage-1 fine-tuning) multi-seed DD OOD evaluation will confirm that the 62pp gap (vanilla 0.284 vs treatment 0.997) is not a seed artifact, and centered CKA will correct the misleading uncentered CKA=1.0 for vanilla vs MLM\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"mf1_binary_seed123\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Binary persuasion classification (seed=123) will maintain DD OOD F1 comparable to seed=42 (0.990), confirming low variance for binary variant and providing 3-seed statistical support for reviewer MF1\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"mf1_binary_seed456\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Binary persuasion DeBERTa+GRU pipeline with seed=456 will produce DD OOD performance consistent with seed=42, confirming reproducibility of the binary collapse approach.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"mf1_scrambled_seed123\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Scrambled persuasion labels (seed=123) will degrade DD OOD detection performance compared to original labels, confirming that persuasion strategy grounding is causally necessary for generalization\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"mf1_scrambled_seed456\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Scrambled-label classification (seed=456) on plan_002_splits data will show DD OOD F1 comparable to seed=42 (0.990), confirming scrambled variant's performance and variance for reviewer MF1\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_003_scrambled_perturbation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Scrambled-label classification (random strategy labels on domain text) will show perturbation degradation between binary (0.000) and MLM (-0.026), testing whether correct label content matters for robustness or if any classification structure suffices\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"plan_003_scrambled_fix\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"If correct persuasion strategy labels are the key mechanism for jailbreak detection, then scrambling these labels (while keeping intent labels intact) should significantly degrade detection performance, especially on OOD data.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"plan_002_seed456\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Reproduce plan_002 with seed=456 to confirm stability of persuasion-grounded DeBERTa detection advantage over vanilla baseline across random seeds\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_002_seed123\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"9-class persuasion treatment seed=123 achieves same DD OOD performance as seed=42 (F1~1.0), confirming low variance in treatment's cross-attack generalization.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_018_wiki_mlm_control\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"If domain-specific text exposure drives OOD generalization (plan_017 showed jailbreak-domain MLM achieves F1=1.0 on DD OOD), then Wikipedia MLM with matched token count should perform significantly worse, proving domain specificity is necessary rather than any continued pretraining.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_017_mlm_control\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"MLM continued pretraining (no classification labels) on the same training text will NOT achieve DD OOD F1 comparable to persuasion fine-tuning, proving that classification supervision (not just text exposure) is necessary for cross-attack generalization\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"plan_013_binary_perturbation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Binary persuasion classification will maintain near-zero perturbation degradation similar to 9-class, proving robustness comes from persuasion-presence detection not fine-grained strategy discrimination\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"plan_016v2_topic_perturbation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Topic classification model will show significant perturbation degradation comparable to vanilla baseline, as non-aligned classification does not teach lexically-invariant features\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"plan_016v2_topic_control\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Topic classification fine-tuning (without intent head) on the same training text will NOT achieve DD OOD F1 comparable to persuasion fine-tuning, proving that persuasion-specific task (not generic classification) drives cross-attack generalization\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"plan_018_wiki_perturbation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Wikipedia MLM model will show significant perturbation degradation (worse than jailbreak MLM -0.026), as out-of-domain pretraining provides even less robustness to surface-level perturbation\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_017_mlm_perturbation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"MLM domain-adapted model will maintain robustness under adversarial perturbation (0pp degradation), matching persuasion treatment and confirming domain adaptation (not classification supervision) drives perturbation robustness\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"plan_012_gpt55_judge\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Zero-shot LLM-as-judge (GPT-5.5) will underperform specialized trained classifiers at early jailbreak detection despite being a stronger LLM than Qwen3-8B, establishing that even frontier LLMs cannot match trained detection models\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_015_dd_eval\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Mean pooling classifier (without temporal modeling) will perform comparably to BiGRU on DD OOD data, proving GRU temporal modeling is not necessary and persuasion embeddings alone drive cross-attack generalization\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_006_cross_attack_clean\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Persuasion-grounded DeBERTa fine-tuning (trained on Crescendo) produces transferable structural signals that generalize to unseen attack types (FITD/DD), outperforming vanilla DeBERTa baseline especially on structurally different attacks like Deceptive Delight\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_004_perturbation_eval\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Persuasion-grounded DeBERTa embeddings are robust to adversarial paraphrasing (aggressive user-turn rewriting), while vanilla DeBERTa baseline will show significant F1 degradation, proving treatment relies on structural persuasion signals rather than surface lexical features\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_013_none_collapse\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Binary persuasion collapse (persuasion-present vs none) will perform comparably to full 9-class persuasion classification, proving the model is fundamentally a persuasion-presence detector rather than relying on fine-grained strategy discrimination\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_012_llm_judge_qwen\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Zero-shot LLM-as-judge (Qwen3-8B) will underperform specialized trained classifiers at early jailbreak detection, establishing a deployed baseline for comparison\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_002_clean_training\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Persuasion-grounded DeBERTa embeddings on topic-anchored data will show significant early detection advantage over vanilla baseline, with Treatment-Baseline gap > 5pp at K=2 or K=3\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"plan_003_scrambled_ablation\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Scrambling persuasion strategy labels while preserving distribution will significantly degrade detection performance, proving strategy labels carry real signal beyond surface confounds\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"cross_attack_baseline\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Persuasion-grounded DeBERTa embeddings (trained on Crescendo) generalize to unseen attack types (FITD/DD) for early detection, outperforming vanilla DeBERTa baseline especially at low K\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"plan_008_strategy_transition\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Crescendo exhibits monotonically escalating strategy sequences (mean index increases over turns) while FITD/DD show distinct transition patterns, supporting the Pragmatic Evasion Asymmetry claim\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_007_tfidf_fitd_dd\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"TF-IDF classifier on FITD/DD vs v4c benign will achieve F1=1.0, confirming v4c benign has topic/vocabulary confound with all attack types (not just Crescendo)\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"plan_005_multi_attack_gen\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"FITD and Deceptive Delight attack conversations can be generated with prescribed strategy sequences using Qwen3-8B, producing 80+80 conversations with distinct strategy distributions from Crescendo\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"phase_b_full\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Persuasion-grounded DeBERTa embeddings (multi-task fine-tuned on 8-class persuasion + intent) enable significantly earlier detection of Crescendo jailbreak attacks compared to vanilla DeBERTa embeddings, especially at low K (2-3 turns)\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}, \\\"pilot_phase_b\\\": {\\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Persuasion-aware DeBERTa embeddings (multi-task fine-tuned) fed to Bi-GRU will outperform vanilla DeBERTa embeddings at early detection (K=2,3,5 turns) of Crescendo-style jailbreak attacks\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\"}}, \\\"count\\\": 102, \\\"total\\\": 102, \\\"baseline\\\": null}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.3613}}\"}",
  "mcpMeta": {
    "structuredContent": {
      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"experiments\": {\"exp65_svd_necessity_ablation\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Removing top-3 SVD directions from 9-class CLS embeddings will significantly degrade OOD detection (necessity), while removing random-3 directions will not, complementing exp54 sufficiency evidence\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp64_toxicchat_confidence_analysis\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class 在 ToxicChat 上的高 FPR 来源于高置信误判（>0.9），而非 borderline 预测，说明 classification DAPT 对 ToxicChat benign 文本产生了系统性的特征偏移而非边界模糊\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp11_tost_equivalence\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Classification-supervised DeBERTa variants (9-class, scrambled, binary) are statistically equivalent on DD OOD (within Δ=5pp margin), formally proving that persuasion label content does not matter — any classification structure on domain text produces equivalent cross-attack generalization.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp46_inference_efficiency\": {\"hypothesis\": \"DisPeD (DeBERTa+BiGRU) achieves 100x+ speedup over LLM-as-judge (Qwen3-8B 3-shot) with comparable or better OOD detection, because the encoder pipeline processes fixed-length embeddings per turn rather than generating full-sequence autoregressive outputs. This quantifies the practical deployment advantage.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp63_toxicchat_fpfn\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class ToxicChat performance gap vs MLM is dominated by false positives (>70%), because classification DAPT over-encodes synthetic distributional features that trigger on human benign text\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp62_topic_diagnosis\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Topic DAPT fails because it blurs the jailbreak/benign boundary in embedding space — topic classification teaches features orthogonal to the jailbreak detection task, resulting in lower Fisher separability between jailbreak and benign representations\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp61_random_binary_partition\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Random binary partitions of 9 strategy labels will achieve comparable OOD generalization to the original binary label, confirming that DAPT learns domain representations rather than label semantics — any domain-relevant binary partition suffices\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp60_turncount_baseline\": {\"hypothesis\": \"A turn-count-only logistic regression will achieve near-chance F1 on all test sets, confirming that user-turn distributions overlap between jailbreak and benign and that detection is content-driven rather than length-driven\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp59_resample_350\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Randomly sampling 350 conversations from expanded_1000 will show similar OOD degradation as the full 1000-sample training, confirming that the performance drop is driven by data composition changes (new attack types/benign sources) rather than sample count\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp58_truncated_single_turn\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Truncating synthetic multi-turn conversations to the last user turn will show classification DAPT maintaining advantage over MLM, indicating the ToxicChat reversal is driven by multi-turn structural dependence rather than single-turn distributional properties\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp57_svd_iid_only\": {\"hypothesis\": \"SVD directions computed on IID training data only (without OOD test exposure) are sufficient for cross-attack OOD detection via logistic probe, eliminating the data leakage concern in exp54's causal subspace analysis\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp53_llm_judge_prompt_opt\": {\"hypothesis\": \"An optimized CoT prompt with structured reasoning steps and calibrated examples will improve LLM-as-Judge jailbreak detection F1, especially on OOD datasets (DD, AA), compared to the original 3-shot prompt\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp55_5seed_mlm_vs_9class_aa\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class persuasion-grounded DeBERTa DAPT produces significantly better OOD generalization than domain-exposure-only MLM DAPT, with the advantage being robust across 5 random seeds (expanding from 3 to address reviewer W2 concern about insufficient seed count)\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp56_crossllm_10shot_adaptation\": {\"hypothesis\": \"10-shot benign adaptation with Llama-origin conversations will reduce cross-LLM FPR from 88-90% to below 10%, extending the two-axis benign adaptation mechanism to cross-LLM distributional shift\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp54_causal_probe_subspace\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Classification DAPT compresses representations into few discriminative dimensions that are causally necessary for OOD generalization - projecting onto top-k SVD components preserves detection while random/vanilla projections fail\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp52_oasst2_n25_multiseed\": {\"hypothesis\": \"OASST2 benign adaptation with N=25 yields consistently low FPR (≤5%) across multiple seeds (42, 123, 456), confirming seed 42's FPR=1.5% is not a lucky outlier.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp51_lmsys_benign_ood\": {\"hypothesis\": \"The two-axis decomposition (high FPR on OOD benign + 10-shot remediation) generalizes beyond WildChat: LMSYS-Chat-1M benign conversations will show high FPR (>20%) that 10 target-domain conversations can eliminate, confirming benign-side shift is a general phenomenon not specific to WildChat GPT-3.5 users.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp50_layerwise_representation\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Discriminative compression from classification DAPT concentrates in DeBERTa's later layers (7-12) rather than early layers (1-6), mechanistically distinguishing it from Maennel et al.'s early-layer label-independent statistics. If PR and Fisher ratio diverge between classification-supervised and MLM/vanilla only in later layers, this confirms compression is a task-specific representation restructuring, not an early-layer phenomenon.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp45_benign_adaptation_curve\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Adding N WildChat benign samples (N in {0,5,10,25,50,100,150}) to training data will show a monotonic FPR reduction curve, with substantial FPR drop achievable with as few as 10-25 samples while maintaining OOD F1 above 0.95.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp49_representation_analysis\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Classification-based DAPT (9-class, binary, scrambled) produces smaller weight perturbations from pretrained weights and more geometrically uniform representations (higher effective dimensionality) than MLM DAPT, consistent with optimization regularization as the primary mechanism for cross-attack robustness.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp48_ensemble_mlm_9class\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Ensembling JB-MLM (strongest on ToxicChat F1=0.837) and 9-class (strongest on synthetic OOD) will outperform either individual model across all benchmarks, because their error patterns are complementary: MLM captures general linguistic anomaly while 9-class captures persuasion-strategy-specific features.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp46\": {\"hypothesis\": \"DisPeD (DeBERTa+BiGRU) achieves significantly lower inference latency than Qwen3-8B LLM-as-judge while maintaining superior accuracy, because the specialized two-stage pipeline avoids autoregressive decoding overhead.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp44_multi_llm_mixed\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Mixed multi-LLM training (Qwen+Llama jailbreak data) maintains evidence hierarchy and OOD detection on cross-LLM test while preserving Qwen OOD F1 above 0.95, because persuasion strategy patterns are LLM-agnostic features\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp43_mhj_human_eval\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class DAPT representations encode transferable persuasion signals that generalize to real human multi-turn jailbreak attempts (MHJ dataset), achieving higher detection recall than vanilla DeBERTa because human attackers use similar persuasion strategies as synthetic attacks.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp42b_balanced_ratio_ablation\": {\"hypothesis\": \"exp42's WildChat FPR reduction (68.1%→0%) is driven by benign distribution coverage, not the shifted class ratio (50:50→65:35). With balanced 50:50 ratio (88 WC benign + 87 orig benign vs 175 jailbreak), FPR should still drop to near 0%.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp42_ood_benign_mixed\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Including WildChat benign conversations in training data will reduce WildChat FPR from 68.1% to below 30% while maintaining jailbreak OOD detection F1 above 0.95 on DD/AA/FITD, because the current high FPR is driven by benign distribution mismatch (exp14c: 100% from FP explosion), not model architecture limitations. Mixed benign training should expand the model's benign representation space to cover real-world conversational diversity.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp41_tost_10seed\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Extending TOST equivalence test from n=5 to n=10 seeds will resolve the inconclusive AA result (p=0.08 at n=5) by providing sufficient statistical power: if scrambled and 9-class are truly equivalent within 5pp margin on ActorAttack, the increased sample size will yield p<0.05, confirming label content irrelevance across all OOD families.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp40_llama_guard_baseline\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Llama Guard 3 8B（single-turn 安全分类器）在多轮 jailbreak 检测上表现远低于 DisPeD，因为它缺乏多轮对话的时序建模能力和说服策略感知，验证 multi-turn 场景需要专门适配的核心 claim。\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp40\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Llama Guard 3 8B, as a general-purpose safety classifier, will underperform on multi-turn persuasion-based jailbreak detection compared to our specialized turn-level model, particularly on early detection (K=2) and OOD attack families, because it lacks turn-level persuasion strategy awareness.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp39_extra_seeds_tost\": {\"hypothesis\": \"扩展 9-class 和 scrambled 到 5 seeds (新增 789/1000) 将提供足够统计效力支持 TOST 等价性检验：scrambled 在 DD OOD 上与 9-class 等价（差距 &lt;2pp），但在 AA OOD 上显著更低，证明策略标签的正确语义信息对 hard OOD 至关重要。\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp38_wildcat_calibration\": {\"hypothesis\": \"少量 WildChat benign 样本 (~50 条) 即可通过阈值校准将 FPR 降至 <25% 且 TPR >90%，证明 domain calibration 是可行的 practical path，WildChat 高 FPR 是阈值问题而非表征能力问题。\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp37_length_matched_benign\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class 模型不依赖 turn count 作为检测 shortcut：截断 benign 对话到 jailbreak 匹配长度 (3-10 turns) 后 FPR 不会显著上升，WildChat 短对话 (3-10 turns) 的 FPR 也不高于长对话，因为 DAPT 训练迫使模型学习说服策略内容特征而非 length 统计量。\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp36\": {\"hypothesis\": \"MLM DAPT encoder produces weaker frozen representations than classification (9-class) DAPT encoder for OOD jailbreak detection, because classification training forces the encoder to learn task-relevant discriminative features while MLM only learns general language patterns.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp35_cross_seed_ensemble\": {\"hypothesis\": \"3-seed majority vote ensemble 将在所有 OOD 攻击家族上提升检测 F1（尤其减少 AA OOD 上的漏检），9-class ensemble 的提升幅度大于 MLM/vanilla，因为 9-class 各 seed 的错误模式更互补（来自不同的策略表征空间初始化）。\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp34_representation_tsne\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class DAPT 的 CLS embedding 在 t-SNE/UMAP 2D 投影中将显示 jailbreak/benign 更清晰的分离（尤其在 AA OOD 上），而 vanilla 和 MLM 的表征将呈现更多重叠，因为 9-class 的说服策略预训练迫使编码器学习与攻击意图相关的判别性特征。\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp33_strategy_prediction_plan002\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Plan_002 checkpoints (主管道, AA OOD ~97%) 的策略预测-检测关联将比 exp32 (exp16 mixed-LLM checkpoints) 更强，因为 plan_002 在 AA OOD 上检测率更高，正确检测样本的策略预测准确率与检测概率的相关性也应更显著。\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp28c_vanilla_supplement\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Vanilla DeBERTa (no DAPT) baseline at N=50 and N=250 will show substantially lower OOD F1 compared to 9-class DAPT, completing the data efficiency curve and confirming that DAPT benefit persists across all training sizes.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp32_strategy_prediction\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Correctly detected jailbreak conversations have higher persuasion strategy prediction accuracy than missed ones, and certain strategy types (e.g., rapport_building, emotional_manipulation) are associated with higher detection rates, because the persuasion head's ability to identify strategies is mechanistically linked to the encoder's discriminative power for downstream detection.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp31_lambda_ablation_aa\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Multi-task persuasion loss weight (lambda) affects OOD generalization in E2E fine-tuning: lambda=0 (intent-only) will underperform on hard OOD (ActorAttack) compared to lambda>0, because the persuasion auxiliary signal provides a regularization effect that preserves OOD-relevant features in the encoder. There exists an optimal lambda that maximizes AA OOD without sacrificing IID performance.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp28b_data_efficiency_supplement\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class DAPT maintains robust OOD performance across different random seeds (model initialization), and vanilla DeBERTa shows poor OOD generalization even at smaller training sizes (N=50, N=250), confirming that the data efficiency advantage of 9-class DAPT is not an artifact of a single seed.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp29_inference_efficiency\": {\"hypothesis\": \"DeBERTa+BiGRU achieves orders-of-magnitude faster inference than LLM-based judges while maintaining superior OOD detection accuracy, demonstrating practical deployability for real-time jailbreak detection.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp28_data_efficiency\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class DAPT maintains strong OOD generalization even at 25% training data (n=50-125), outperforming vanilla baseline at 100% data (n=350), demonstrating high sample efficiency of persuasion-grounded representations.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp30_length_stratified\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class DAPT maintains consistent jailbreak detection performance across different conversation lengths (turn counts), with no systematic degradation in shorter or longer conversations.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp27_mean_pooling\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Mean pooling (no temporal modeling) with end-to-end fine-tuning will match BiGRU E2E on easy OOD (DD) but degrade on hard OOD (ActorAttack), demonstrating that temporal modeling via BiGRU is necessary for detecting multi-turn adaptive attacks.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp26_frozen_probing\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Frozen 9-class DAPT DeBERTa representations alone carry sufficient OOD generalization signal: a randomly-initialized BiGRU trained on these frozen embeddings should achieve comparable OOD F1 to the jointly-trained plan_002 GRU, because the DAPT pre-training encodes persuasion-strategy structure that transfers across attack types.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp24_calibration_ece\": {\"hypothesis\": \"The 9-class multi-task model produces better-calibrated confidence scores than vanilla DeBERTa+BiGRU, especially under OOD distribution shift, because persuasion-aware features provide more grounded probability estimates.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp22_aa_early_detection\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class DAPT maintains detection advantage over MLM and vanilla at all prefix lengths (K=1,2,3,5,full) on ActorAttack OOD, with the gap widening at smaller K, demonstrating early detection capability on hard OOD attacks\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp25_fitd_early_detection\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class persuasion-aware model detects FITD jailbreaks earlier (higher F1 at low K) than MLM and vanilla baselines, similar to the pattern observed on ActorAttack OOD.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp23_aa_error_analysis\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class DAPT errors on AA OOD are concentrated in specific topics/strategies, with short-turn conversations being harder to detect\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp21_cross_checkpoint\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Downstream OOD performance is insensitive to Stage-1 DAPT training variation: different DAPT seeds should yield cross-checkpoint F1 std < 2pp on IID, DD OOD, and AA OOD benchmarks.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp20_deepcontext_proxy\": {\"hypothesis\": \"BERT-base-uncased + BiGRU (frozen probing, no DAPT) as DeepContext proxy will substantially underperform DeBERTa-v3 9-class on OOD generalization, quantifying the contribution of task-specific DAPT vs generic pre-training and providing a SOTA-comparable baseline for reviewer concern about missing baselines\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp19_wildchat_fpr\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class DAPT classifier maintains FPR < 15% on high-drift real-world benign conversations from WildChat (after excluding jailbreak-contaminated samples), demonstrating external validity beyond synthetic benign data and addressing reviewer concern about deployment false positive rates\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp18_e2e_finetune\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Unfreezing DeBERTa-v3-base encoder for end-to-end fine-tuning (Stage 1 DAPT + Stage 2 GRU joint training) will produce comparable or better OOD F1 vs frozen probing, quantifying the performance gap of the frozen approach and validating probing methodology\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp16_multi_llm\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Training on mixed Qwen+Llama conversations (250+250) eliminates single-LLM confound: the evidence hierarchy (9-class >> MLM >> vanilla) holds on mixed data, and cross-LLM false positive rate drops vs single-source training, proving DAPT generalizes across LLM distributions\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp17_dataset_scale\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Doubling training data from 500 to 1000 conversations (with new topic seeds) will maintain or improve the DAPT evidence hierarchy (9-class >> MLM >> vanilla on OOD), demonstrating that results are not an artifact of small sample size.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp16b_llama_only_mlm\": {\"hypothesis\": \"MLM DAPT on Llama-only data (250 conversations) will produce stable intermediate performance between vanilla and 9-class on DD OOD and AA OOD, distinguishing whether MLM instability in exp16 mixed training is caused by multi-source data mixing or is inherent to MLM DAPT\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp15c_roberta_large_mlm\": {\"hypothesis\": \"MLM-only continued pretraining on RoBERTa-large (without classification head) will NOT cause the same performance regression as 9-class DAPT, because the regression stems from classification fine-tuning conflicting with frozen-encoder GRU evaluation, not from domain adaptation itself.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp14c_prediction_analysis\": {\"hypothesis\": \"exp14 cross-LLM failure is driven by high false positive rate on Llama benign (model classifies unfamiliar Llama benign text as jailbreak). exp14b control should show near-zero FP on Qwen benign with maintained TP on Llama jailbreak, confirming benign distribution coverage as the bottleneck.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp15b_roberta_large_lr\": {\"hypothesis\": \"exp15 9class underperformance on AA (0.946 vs vanilla 1.000) is caused by catastrophic forgetting from too-high LR (2e-5) for 355M model. Reducing DAPT LR to 1e-5 should preserve pretrained representations while still learning persuasion patterns, recovering AA F1 to match or exceed vanilla.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp15_roberta_large\": {\"hypothesis\": \"RoBERTa-large (355M) vanilla will still underperform DAPT variants on ActorAttack OOD, demonstrating that DAPT value is independent of model capacity. If RoBERTa-large vanilla matches DAPT performance, then the difficulty threshold is a capacity effect rather than a representation quality effect.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp14b_cross_llm_control\": {\"hypothesis\": \"exp14 cross-LLM failure is caused by double distribution shift (both jailbreak AND benign from Llama). By pairing Llama jailbreak with Qwen IID benign, we isolate the jailbreak detection component. If F1 recovers significantly, the failure is primarily due to Llama benign misclassification, not inability to detect Llama-style jailbreaks.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp14_cross_llm_validation\": {\"hypothesis\": \"DAPT advantage (9-class/jb_mlm >> vanilla) is not specific to Qwen3-8B-generated data. If the persuasion-grounded fine-tuning captures generalizable jailbreak patterns rather than Qwen-specific artifacts, the performance gap should persist on Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct-generated multi-turn conversations.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp5_llm_judge_ood\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Zero-shot Qwen3-8B will underperform trained classifiers on OOD data (DD and FITD), establishing that even on unseen attack types, specialized trained detectors outperform general-purpose LLM reasoning\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp9_bootstrap_ci\": {\"hypothesis\": \"BCa bootstrap CIs and paired permutation tests will confirm that multitask 9-class persuasion pre-training significantly outperforms vanilla (frozen) baseline on OOD jailbreak detection, while the difference against domain-specific pre-training (jb_mlm, scrambled) is not statistically significant, supporting the claim that persuasion-aware representations provide robust detection capabilities.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp8_mhj_human_data\": {\"hypothesis\": \"If the evidence hierarchy (domain-adapted > vanilla) reflects genuine signal learning rather than Qwen3-8B stylistic overfitting, then DeBERTa variants trained on Qwen3-8B data should also outperform vanilla on human-written jailbreak prompts from Reddit/Discord.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp10_fewshot_llm_judge\": {\"hypothesis\": \"3-shot in-context examples should significantly improve LLM-as-judge jailbreak detection over zero-shot by providing the model with concrete examples of escalation patterns, but will still underperform trained classifiers because few-shot prompting cannot match fine-tuned feature extraction.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp7_tfidf_perprefix_actorattack\": {\"hypothesis\": \"TF-IDF LR baseline per-prefix results on DD OOD and ActorAttack will show the complete performance profile across K values, filling the gap where only Full F1 was reported, and establishing the full comparison surface against neural methods\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp6_actorattack_perturbation\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Classification-supervised variants (9-class/binary/scrambled) will maintain near-zero perturbation degradation on ActorAttack OOD paraphrased data, while vanilla/MLM variants will show significant degradation, extending Claim 3 perturbation robustness from DD to a second OOD attack type\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp2_actorattack_mcnemar\": {\"hypothesis\": \"9-class persuasion-aware encoder significantly outperforms vanilla DeBERTa on ActorAttack OOD data at per-sample level (McNemar test), supporting cross-attack generalization claim.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp2_actorattack_ood\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Persuasion-grounded DeBERTa will generalize to ActorAttack (a structurally distinct multi-persona attack), extending cross-attack OOD evidence beyond DD and FITD to a third unseen attack type\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp4_dd_ood_perturbation\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Persuasion-grounded DeBERTa variants will maintain near-zero perturbation degradation on DD OOD paraphrased data, while vanilla/wiki-MLM variants will show significant degradation, extending IID perturbation robustness findings to OOD setting\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp3_roberta_probing\": {\"hypothesis\": \"RoBERTa 9-class persuasion encoder encodes richer discourse structure than vanilla/MLM variants (matching DeBERTa pattern), as measured by linear probing accuracy and CKA divergence, confirming findings are encoder-agnostic\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"vanilla_multiseed_dd_ood\": {\"hypothesis\": \"DeBERTa vanilla (no Stage-1 fine-tuning) multi-seed DD OOD evaluation will confirm that the 62pp gap (vanilla 0.284 vs treatment 0.997) is not a seed artifact, and centered CKA will correct the misleading uncentered CKA=1.0 for vanilla vs MLM\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"mf1_binary_seed123\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Binary persuasion classification (seed=123) will maintain DD OOD F1 comparable to seed=42 (0.990), confirming low variance for binary variant and providing 3-seed statistical support for reviewer MF1\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"mf1_binary_seed456\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Binary persuasion DeBERTa+GRU pipeline with seed=456 will produce DD OOD performance consistent with seed=42, confirming reproducibility of the binary collapse approach.\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"mf1_scrambled_seed123\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Scrambled persuasion labels (seed=123) will degrade DD OOD detection performance compared to original labels, confirming that persuasion strategy grounding is causally necessary for generalization\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"mf1_scrambled_seed456\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Scrambled-label classification (seed=456) on plan_002_splits data will show DD OOD F1 comparable to seed=42 (0.990), confirming scrambled variant's performance and variance for reviewer MF1\", \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"plan_003_scrambled_perturbation\": {\"hypothesis\": \"Scrambled-label classification (random strategy labels on domain text) will show perturbation degradation between binary (0.000) and MLM (-0.026), testing whether correct label content matters for robustness or if any classification structure suffices\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\"},
